[SYSTEM]
You are an RTL (Register Transfer Level) design agent. Your task is to create a Verilog/SystemVerilog design that satisfies the given specification, is functionally correct, and has minimal cost (area_delay_product).

**Cost metric `area_delay_product`:** area × achieved critical-path delay — the classic area-delay product. Does NOT include simulation cycle count (contrast with the 'area_runtime_product' metric). Cost is measured on your design alone. The testbench and everything it owns (checking logic, the external memories, etc.) is not synthesized and contributes nothing to area, delay, power, or energy.

## Specification
Full 8x8 2-D IDCT of a baseline JPEG decoder (ultraembedded/core_jpeg), module
name `jpeg_idct_2d`: the row pass (jpeg_idct_x, OUT_SHIFT=11), a double-buffered
corner-turn (jpeg_idct_transpose with four 32x32 dual-port RAMs), and the column
pass (jpeg_idct_y, OUT_SHIFT=15), composed exactly as in the original decoder
(downstream ready tied high; the row pass accepts no backpressure). Each 1-D
pass is the factorized (AAN-style) algorithm with cosine constants scaled by
4096 (C1..C7 = 4017, 3784, 3406, 2896, 2276, 1567, 799), time-sharing 5
multipliers over an 8-beat schedule. ALL arithmetic is evaluated in 32-bit
signed context with wrap-around; outputs of each pass are arithmetic-shifted
right (11 then 15); the 1/sqrt(2) odd-part scaling is `(x * 181) / 256` where
`*` wraps at 32 bits and `/` is SIGNED division truncating toward zero. This
exact fixed-point behavior is what the golden vectors encode.

Interface (clocked, sync active-high reset `rst`; no backpressure):
  inputs : clk, rst, img_start_i (1-cycle pulse resets the output streamers),
           img_end_i (unused), inport_valid_i, inport_idx_i[2:0],
           inport_data0..3_i[15:0]
  outputs: outport_valid_o, outport_data_o[31:0], outport_idx_o[5:0]

Input protocol (the upstream block serializer's FIXED schedule, reproduced
exactly by the testbench): per row of 8 coefficients c0..c7 (16-bit signed),
8 back-to-back valid beats with idx = 0..7; the four data lanes carry the EVEN
set (c0,c2,c4,c6) on beats idx in {0,2,5,7} and the ODD set (c1,c3,c5,c7) on
beats idx in {1,3,4,6}. 8 rows back-to-back form a 64-beat block; between
blocks there are >= 2 idle cycles (valid=0, idx=0, data=0). Pipeline registers
advance every clock regardless of valid — designs must be robust to the gap
pattern exactly as the original is.

Output (fixed schedule): 64 values per block streamed on outport_valid_o; the
k-th streamed value of a block carries outport_idx_o = {k[2:0], k[5:3]} (the
column pass emits column-major; the idx label gives the ROW-MAJOR pixel
position) and must match the expected output at that position. The stream of a
block overlaps the next block's input beats (the transpose double-buffers one
full block).

A working starting point is provided: `starting_point.v` contains the
ORIGINAL core_jpeg modules (Apache 2.0), unmodified, under a thin composition
top. It passes all golden vectors (they were generated from it — the original
RTL is the correctness oracle). Study it, then optimize while preserving its
exact behavior. Sibling benchmark: `jpeg_idct_2d_spire` (identical tb.sv +
vectors.dat, bit-exact SpireHDL starting point). The 1-D row pass alone is
also available as the `jpeg_idct_x_*` benchmarks.

NOTE on memories: the cost-evaluation synthesis flow targets a standard-cell
library with no RAM macros, so every behavioral memory in this design (the
transpose buffers, block_out, etc.) is inferred as a register array plus mux
decode REGARDLESS of coding style. Optimizing that decode/mux logic is fair
game; instantiating a hard RAM macro is not possible in this flow.

## Tools
- create_file: Create a new file in your working directory
- replace_file: Replace the entire contents of an existing file
- apply_diff: Apply a unified diff to modify a file
- edit_file: Edit a file by replacing an exact string match with new content. Provide old_string (must match exactly once) and new_string.
- ls: List files in your working directory
- read_file: Read the contents of a file
- run_evaluation(filename[, target_delay]): Run evaluation on the given design file. Returns correctness (Verilator simulation) + cost (Yosys area_delay_product). The filename must be your main design file (e.g. 'design.sv'). Optionally pass target_delay (ps) to override the synthesis timing constraint.

## Strategy
1. Lay out an action plan. Try to cover a diverse set of approaches in your plan to increase the chances of finding a good solution within the step limit. Also once you find a new best solution, explore close solutions. Trade off exploration with exploitation.
2. First, create a simple, straightforward design that is functionally correct. Focus on getting 100% correctness first.
3. Run evaluation to confirm correctness and get the initial cost (area_delay_product).
4. ONLY after achieving 100% correctness, try to optimize the design to reduce the area_delay_product.
5. After each optimization, run evaluation to verify correctness is maintained and check the new cost.
6. If an optimization breaks correctness, revert and try a different approach.
7. Keep iterating until you run out of steps — use every step to try improvements. The run ends automatically at the step limit; your best passing design is kept.

In general, be creative and take risk. At least after you have a correct design, try bold optimizations to reduce area_delay_product, even if they might break correctness. You can always revert if it doesn't work.
The best results you obtain which is 100% correctness counts.
Try to beat at least the straightforward implementation.

Running evaluation is crucial after every change to ensure correctness and track cost. Do not skip evaluations.

You may do some 'thinking' alongside with your tool calling responses to explain your reasoning, but always remember to call a tool in every response.

## Important
- Always call a tool in every response.
- You have a maximum of **60 steps** (LLM turns). Budget them wisely: get a correct design first, then optimise. You may batch tool calls in one response --- at most 8, and only one `run_evaluation`.
- The best result is the one with lowest cost among designs that pass 100% of testbench checks.
- Use different filenames for different design variants so you can retrieve them later.
- If context files (e.g. component source files) are in your workspace, you can modify them directly using edit_file or replace_file to optimize the design. Make targeted, incremental changes rather than rewriting files from scratch — small edits are less likely to introduce bugs.
- Write clean, synthesizable RTL code.
- You can pass `target_delay` (ps) to `run_evaluation` to try different synthesis timing constraints; experimenting with this may improve PPA results (lower target delay may reduce delay, higher target delay may reduce area/transistor count). target_delay must be > 0.
- Do not spend too many steps sweeping `target_delay` — basically if you want to minimize delay choose a low value,if you want to minimize area/transistor count choose a high value. That's it.A few targeted experiments are more productive than an exhaustive sweep.

## Delay Optimization Tips
- `target_delay` trades area against delay (a lower value pressures toward faster logic, a higher value lets Yosys re-map to smaller cells). Do not overly iterate on different target delays; often the RTL structure is the real lever, target_delay is only a minor secondary knob.
- After getting a correct design, try different accumulation orderings (sequential vs. tree) and compare results — Yosys synthesis can produce noticeably different delays (~10 ps) from structurally similar descriptions.
- For designs dominated by a multiply-accumulate chain, the critical path is the multiplier followed by the adder tree. Reducing the adder depth or splitting into parallel partial sums can help.

## Reference Implementations
The following are reference implementations for inspiration. Study them for patterns and techniques, but do not copy them directly — adapt ideas to your specific design.

### Parallel-prefix adder (ripple-carry / carry-lookahead) (Add.sv)
```systemverilog
// Copyright 2024 ETH Zurich and University of Bologna.
// Solderpad Hardware License, Version 0.51, see LICENSE for details.
// SPDX-License-Identifier: SHL-0.51
//
// Based on the work by Reto Zimmermann 1998 - ETH Zürich
// Originally written in VHDL, available under: 
// https://iis-people.ee.ethz.ch/~zimmi/arith_lib.html#library
//
// Authors:
// - Thomas Benz <tbenz@iis.ee.ethz.ch>
// - Philippe Sauter <phsauter@iis.ee.ethz.ch>
// - Paul Scheffler <paulsc@iis.ee.ethz.ch>
//
// Description :
// Binary adder using ripple-carry or parallel-prefix carry-lookahead logic.
// S = A+B

module Add #(
	parameter int              width = 8,             // word width
	parameter lau_pkg::speed_e speed = lau_pkg::FAST  // performance parameter
) (
	input  logic [width-1:0] A,  // operands
	input  logic [width-1:0] B,
	output logic [width-1:0] S   // sum
);

	// Function: Binary adder using parallel-prefix carry-lookahead logic.

	logic [width-1:0] GI, PI;  // prefix gen./prop. in
	logic [width-1:0] GO, PO;  // prefix gen./prop. out
	logic [width-1:0] PT;  // adder propagate temp

	// Internal signals for unsigned operands
	logic [width-1:0] Auns, Buns, Suns;

	// default ripple-carry adder as slow implementation
	if (speed == lau_pkg::SLOW) begin
		// type conversion: std_logic_vector -> unsigned
		assign Auns = A;
		assign Buns = B;

		// addition
		assign Suns = Auns + Buns;

		// type conversion: unsigned -> std_logic_vector
		assign S = Suns;
	end else begin
		// parallel-prefix adders as medium and fast implementations

		// calculate prefix input generate/propagate signals
		assign GI = A & B;
		assign PI = A | B;
		// calculate adder propagate signals (PT = A xor B)
		assign PT = ~GI & PI;

		// calculate prefix output generate/propagate signals
		PrefixAndOr #(
			.width(width),
			.speed(speed)
		) prefix (
			.GI(GI),
			.PI(PI),
			.GO(GO),
			.PO(PO)
		);

		// calculate sum bits
		assign S = PT ^ {GO[width-2:0], 1'b0};
	end
endmodule



module behavioural_Add #(
	parameter int              width = 8,             // word width
	parameter lau_pkg::speed_e speed = lau_pkg::FAST  // performance parameter
) (
	input  logic [width-1:0] A,  // operands
	input  logic [width-1:0] B,
	output logic [width-1:0] S   // sum
);
	assign S = A + B;
endmodule
```

### Prefix AND-OR structures (Sklansky, Brent-Kung, serial) (PrefixAndOr.sv)
```systemverilog
// Copyright 2024 ETH Zurich and University of Bologna.
// Solderpad Hardware License, Version 0.51, see LICENSE for details.
// SPDX-License-Identifier: SHL-0.51
//
// Based on the work by Reto Zimmermann 1998 - ETH Zürich
// Originally written in VHDL, available under: 
// https://iis-people.ee.ethz.ch/~zimmi/arith_lib.html#library
//
// Authors:
// - Thomas Benz <tbenz@iis.ee.ethz.ch>
// - Philippe Sauter <phsauter@iis.ee.ethz.ch>
// - Paul Scheffler <paulsc@iis.ee.ethz.ch>
//
// Description :
// Prefix structures of different depth (i.e. speed) for carry calculation
// in binary adders. Compute in m levels new generate/propagate signal pairs
// for always larger groups of bits. Generate signals of the last level correspond
// to carries in binary addition. Basic logic operations: AND-OR for generate
// signals, AND for propagate signals.

module PrefixAndOr #(
	parameter int              width = 8,             // word width
	parameter lau_pkg::speed_e speed = lau_pkg::FAST  // performance parameter
) (
	input  logic [width-1:0] GI,  // gen./prop. in
	input  logic [width-1:0] PI,  // gen./prop. in
	output logic [width-1:0] GO,  // gen./prop. out
	output logic [width-1:0] PO   // gen./prop. out
);

	// Constants
	localparam int n = width;  // prefix structure width
	localparam int m = $clog2(width);  // prefix structure depth

	// Sklansky parallel-prefix carry-lookahead structure
	if (speed == lau_pkg::FAST) begin : fastPrefix
		logic [(m+1)*n-1:0] GT, PT;  // gen./prop. temp
			assign GT[n-1:0] = GI;
			assign PT[n-1:0] = PI;
			for (genvar l = 1; l <= m; l++) begin : levels
				for (genvar k = 0; k < 2 ** (m - l); k++) begin : groups
					for (genvar i = 0; i < 2 ** (l - 1); i++) begin : bits
						// pass prop and gen to following nodes
						if ((k * 2 ** l + i) < n) begin : white
							assign GT[l*n+k*2**l+i] = GT[(l-1)*n+k*2**l+i];
							assign PT[l*n+k*2**l+i] = PT[(l-1)*n+k*2**l+i];
						end
						// calculate new propagate and generate
						if ((k * 2 ** l + 2 ** (l - 1) + i) < n) begin : black
							assign GT[l*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) + i] = 
												GT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) + i]
											  | (  PT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) + i]
												 & GT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) - 1] );
							assign PT[l*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) + i] = 
													PT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) + i]
												  & PT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) - 1];
						end
					end
				end
			end
			assign GO = GT[(m+1)*n-1 : m*n];
			assign PO = PT[(m+1)*n-1 : m*n];
	end

	// Brent-Kung parallel-prefix carry-lookahead structure
	if (speed == lau_pkg::MEDIUM) begin : mediumPrefix
		logic [(2*m)*n -1:0] GT, PT;  // gen./prop. temp
		assign GT[n-1:0] = GI;
		assign PT[n-1:0] = PI;

		for (genvar l = 1; l <= m; l++) begin : levels1
			for (genvar k = 0; k < 2**(m-l); k++) begin : groups
				for (genvar i = 0; i < 2**l -1; i++) begin : bits
					if ((k* 2**l +i) < n) begin : white
						assign GT[l*n + k* 2**l +i] = GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**l +i];
						assign PT[l*n + k* 2**l +i] = PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**l +i];
					end // white
				end // bits
				if ((k* 2**l + 2**l -1) < n) begin : black
					assign GT[l*n + k* 2**l + 2**l -1] =
								GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**l + 2**l - 1] |
							  | (  PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**l + 2**l     -1] 
							     & GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**l + 2**(l-1) -1]);
					assign PT[l*n + k* 2**l + 2**l -1] =
								PT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**l     -1] 
							  & PT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) -1];
				end // black
			end
		end // level1
		for (genvar l = m +1; l < 2*m; l++) begin : levels2
			for (genvar i = 0; i < 2**(2*m -l); i++) begin : bits
				if (i < n) begin : white
					assign GT[l*n +i] = GT[(l-1)*n +i];
					assign PT[l*n +i] = PT[(l-1)*n +i];
				end // white
			end // bits
			for (genvar k = 1; k < 2**(l-m); k++) begin : groups
				if (l < 2*m -1) begin : empty
					for (genvar i = 0; i < 2**(2*m -l -1) -1; i++) begin : bits
						if ((k* 2**(2*m -l) +i) < n) begin : white
							assign GT[l*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i] = GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i];
							assign PT[l*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i] = PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i];
						end // white
					end
				end // empty
				if ((k* 2**(2*m -l) + 2**(2*m -l -1) -1) < n) begin : black
					assign GT[l*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) + 2**(2*m -l-1) -1] = 
								GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m-l) + 2**(2*m -l-1) -1]
							  | (  PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m-l) + 2**(2*m-l-1) -1] 
								 & GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m-l) -1] );
					assign PT[l*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) + 2**(2*m -l-1) -1] = 
								PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) + 2**(2*m -l-1) -1]
							  & PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) -1];
				end // black
				for (genvar i = 2**(2*m -l-1); i < 2**(2*m -l); i++) begin : bits
					if ((k* 2**(2*m -l) +i) < n) begin : white
						assign GT[l*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i] = GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i];
						assign PT[l*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i] = PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i];
					end // white
				end
			end
		end // level2
		assign GO = GT[2*m*n -1 : (2*m -1) * n];
		assign PO = PT[2*m*n -1 : (2*m -1) * n];
	end  // Serial-prefix carry-lookahead structure
	else if (speed == lau_pkg::SLOW) begin : slowPrefix
		logic [n-1:0] GT, PT;  // gen./prop. temp
		assign GT[0] = GI[0];
		assign PT[0] = PI[0];
		
		for (genvar i = 1; i < n; i++) begin : bits
			assign GT[i] = GI[i] | (PI[i] & GT[i-1]);
			assign PT[i] = PI[i] & PT[i-1];
		end
		assign GO = GT;
		assign PO = PT;
	end

endmodule

```

wire signed [31:0] block_in_0_1 = {{16{inport_data0_i[15]}}, inport_data0_i};
wire signed [31:0] block_in_2_3 = {{16{inport_data1_i[15]}}, inport_data1_i};
wire signed [31:0] block_in_4_5 = {{16{inport_data2_i[15]}}, inport_data2_i};
wire signed [31:0] block_in_6_7 = {{16{inport_data3_i[15]}}, inport_data3_i};

//-----------------------------------------------------------------
// IDCT
//-----------------------------------------------------------------
reg signed [31:0] i0;
reg signed [31:0] mul0_a;
reg signed [31:0] mul0_b;
reg signed [31:0] mul1_a;
reg signed [31:0] mul1_b;
reg signed [31:0] mul2_a;
reg signed [31:0] mul2_b;
reg signed [31:0] mul3_a;
reg signed [31:0] mul3_b;
reg signed [31:0] mul4_a;
reg signed [31:0] mul4_b;

always @ (posedge clk )
if (rst)
begin
    i0     <= 32'b0;
    mul0_a <= 32'b0;
    mul0_b <= 32'b0;
    mul1_a <= 32'b0;
    mul1_b <= 32'b0;
    mul2_a <= 32'b0;
    mul2_b <= 32'b0;
    mul3_a <= 32'b0;
    mul3_b <= 32'b0;
    mul4_a <= 32'b0;
    mul4_b <= 32'b0;
end
else
begin
    /* verilator lint_off WIDTH */
    case (inport_idx_i)
    3'd0:
    begin
        i0     <= block_in_0_1 + block_in_4_5;
        mul0_a <= block_in_2_3;
        mul0_b <= C2_16;
        mul1_a <= block_in_6_7;
        mul1_b <= C6_16;
    end
    3'd1:
    begin
        mul0_a <= block_in_0_1;
        mul0_b <= C1_16;
        mul1_a <= block_in_6_7;
        mul1_b <= C7_16;
        mul2_a <= block_in_4_5;
        mul2_b <= C5_16;
        mul3_a <= block_in_2_3;
        mul3_b <= C3_16;
        mul4_a <= i0;
        mul4_b <= C4_16;
    end
    3'd2:
    begin
        i0     <= block_in_0_1 - block_in_4_5;
    end
    3'd3:
    begin
        mul0_a <= block_in_0_1;
        mul0_b <= C7_16;
        mul1_a <= block_in_6_7;
        mul1_b <= C1_16;
        mul2_a <= block_in_4_5;
        mul2_b <= C3_16;
        mul3_a <= block_in_2_3;
        mul3_b <= C5_16;
    end
    3'd4:
    begin
        mul0_a <= block_in_0_1;
        mul0_b <= C7_16;
        mul1_a <= block_in_6_7;
        mul1_b <= C1_16;
        mul2_a <= block_in_4_5;
        mul2_b <= C3_16;
        mul3_a <= block_in_2_3;
        mul3_b <= C5_16;
    end
    3'd5:
    begin
        mul0_a <= block_in_2_3;
        mul0_b <= C6_16;
        mul1_a <= block_in_6_7;
        mul1_b <= C2_16;
        mul4_a <= i0;
        mul4_b <= C4_16;
    end
    default:
        ;
    endcase
    /* verilator lint_on WIDTH */
end

reg signed [31:0] mul0_q;
reg signed [31:0] mul1_q;
reg signed [31:0] mul2_q;
reg signed [31:0] mul3_q;
reg signed [31:0] mul4_q;

always @ (posedge clk )
if (rst)
begin
    mul0_q <= 32'b0;
    mul1_q <= 32'b0;
    mul2_q <= 32'b0;
    mul3_q <= 32'b0;
    mul4_q <= 32'b0;
end
else
begin
    mul0_q <= mul0_a * mul0_b;
    mul1_q <= mul1_a * mul1_b;
    mul2_q <= mul2_a * mul2_b;
    mul3_q <= mul3_a * mul3_b;
    mul4_q <= mul4_a * mul4_b;
end

reg signed [31:0] mul0;
reg signed [31:0] mul1;
reg signed [31:0] mul2;
reg signed [31:0] mul3;
reg signed [31:0] mul4;

always @ (posedge clk )
if (rst)
begin
    mul0 <= 32'b0;
    mul1 <= 32'b0;
    mul2 <= 32'b0;
    mul3 <= 32'b0;
    mul4 <= 32'b0;
end
else
begin
    mul0 <= mul0_q;
    mul1 <= mul1_q;
    mul2 <= mul2_q;
    mul3 <= mul3_q;
    mul4 <= mul4_q;
end

reg        out_stg0_valid_q;
reg [2:0]  out_stg0_idx_q;

always @ (posedge clk )
if (rst)
begin
    out_stg0_valid_q <= 1'b0;
    out_stg0_idx_q   <= 3'b0;
end
else
begin
    out_stg0_valid_q <= inport_valid_i;
    out_stg0_idx_q   <= inport_idx_i;
end

reg        out_stg1_valid_q;
reg [2:0]  out_stg1_idx_q;

always @ (posedge clk )
if (rst)
begin
    out_stg1_valid_q <= 1'b0;
    out_stg1_idx_q   <= 3'b0;
end
else
begin
    out_stg1_valid_q <= out_stg0_valid_q;
    out_stg1_idx_q   <= out_stg0_idx_q;
end

reg        out_stg2_valid_q;
reg [2:0]  out_stg2_idx_q;

always @ (posedge clk )
if (rst)
begin
    out_stg2_valid_q <= 1'b0;
    out_stg2_idx_q   <= 3'b0;
end
else
begin
    out_stg2_valid_q <= out_stg1_valid_q;
    out_stg2_idx_q   <= out_stg1_idx_q;
end

reg signed [31:0] o_s5;
reg signed [31:0] o_s6;
reg signed [31:0] o_s7;
reg signed [31:0] o_t0;
reg signed [31:0] o_t1;
reg signed [31:0] o_t2;
reg signed [31:0] o_t3;
reg signed [31:0] o_t4;
reg signed [31:0] o_t5;
reg signed [31:0] o_t6;
reg signed [31:0] o_t7;
reg signed [31:0] o_t6_5;
reg signed [31:0] o_t5_6;

always @ (posedge clk )
if (rst)
begin
    o_s5   <= 32'b0;
    o_s6   <= 32'b0;
    o_s7   <= 32'b0;
    o_t0   <= 32'b0;
    o_t1   <= 32'b0;
    o_t2   <= 32'b0;
    o_t3   <= 32'b0;
    o_t4   <= 32'b0;
    o_t5   <= 32'b0;
    o_t6   <= 32'b0;
    o_t7   <= 32'b0;
    o_t6_5 <= 32'b0;
    o_t5_6 <= 32'b0;
end
else
begin
    case (out_stg2_idx_q)
    3'd0:
    begin
        o_t3 <= mul0 + mul1; // s3
    end
    3'd1:
    begin
        o_s7 <= mul0 + mul1;
        o_s6 <= mul2 + mul3;
        o_t0 <= mul4;        // s0
    end
    3'd2:
    begin
        o_t0 <= o_t0 + o_t3; // t0
        o_t3 <= o_t0 - o_t3; // t3
        o_t7 <= o_s6 + o_s7;
    end
    3'd3:
    begin
        o_t4 <= (mul0 - mul1) + (mul2 - mul3);
    end
    3'd4:
    begin
        o_t0 <= mul0 - mul1; // s4
        o_s5 <= mul2 - mul3;    
    end
    3'd5:
    begin
        o_t3 <= mul0 - mul1; // s2
        o_t4 <= mul4; // s1
        o_t5 <= o_t0 - o_s5;
        o_t6 <= o_s7 - o_s6;
    end
    3'd6:
    begin
        o_t1 <= o_t4 + o_t3;
        o_t2 <= o_t4 - o_t3;
        o_t6_5 <= o_t6 - o_t5;
        o_t5_6 <= o_t5 + o_t6;
    end
    default:
    begin
        o_s5 <= (o_t6_5 * 181) / 256; // 1/sqrt(2)
        o_s6 <= (o_t5_6 * 181) / 256; // 1/sqrt(2)
    end
    endcase
end

reg        out_stg3_valid_q;
reg [2:0]  out_stg3_idx_q;

always @ (posedge clk )
if (rst)
begin
    out_stg3_valid_q <= 1'b0;
    out_stg3_idx_q   <= 3'b0;
end
else
begin
    out_stg3_valid_q <= out_stg2_valid_q;
    out_stg3_idx_q   <= out_stg2_idx_q;
end

reg signed [31:0] block_out[0:7];
reg signed [31:0] block_out_tmp;

always @ (posedge clk )
if (rst)
begin
    block_out[0] <= 32'b0;
    block_out[1] <= 32'b0;
    block_out[2] <= 32'b0;
    block_out[3] <= 32'b0;
    block_out[4] <= 32'b0;
    block_out[5] <= 32'b0;
    block_out[6] <= 32'b0;
    block_out[7] <= 32'b0;
    block_out_tmp <= 32'b0;
end
else if (out_stg3_valid_q)
begin
    if (out_stg3_idx_q == 3'd3)
    begin
        block_out[0] <= ((o_t0 + o_t7) >>> OUT_SHIFT);
        block_out_tmp <= ((o_t0 - o_t7) >>> OUT_SHIFT); // block_out[7]
        block_out[3] <= ((o_t3 + o_t4) >>> OUT_SHIFT);
        block_out[4] <= ((o_t3 - o_t4) >>> OUT_SHIFT);
    end

    if (out_stg3_idx_q == 3'd6)
        block_out[7] <= block_out_tmp;

    if (out_stg3_idx_q == 3'd7)
    begin
        block_out[2] <= ((o_t2 + o_s5) >>> OUT_SHIFT);
        block_out[5] <= ((o_t2 - o_s5) >>> OUT_SHIFT);
        block_out[1] <= ((o_t1 + o_s6) >>> OUT_SHIFT);
        block_out[6] <= ((o_t1 - o_s6) >>> OUT_SHIFT);
    end
end

reg [7:0] valid_q;

always @ (posedge clk )
if (rst)
    valid_q  <= 8'b0;
else if (img_start_i)
    valid_q  <= 8'b0;
else
    valid_q <= {valid_q[6:0], out_stg3_valid_q};

reg [5:0] ptr_q;

always @ (posedge clk )
if (rst)
    ptr_q <= 6'd0;
else if (img_start_i)
    ptr_q <= 6'd0;
else if (outport_valid_o)
    ptr_q <= ptr_q + 6'd1;

assign outport_valid_o = valid_q[6];
assign outport_data_o  = block_out[ptr_q[2:0]];

assign outport_idx_o   = ptr_q;



endmodule
//-----------------------------------------------------------------
//                      Baseline JPEG Decoder
//                             V0.1
//                       Ultra-Embedded.com

module jpeg_idct_transpose
(
    // Inputs
     input           clk
    ,input           rst
    ,input           img_start_i
    ,input           img_end_i
    ,input           inport_valid_i
    ,input  [ 31:0]  inport_data_i
    ,input  [  5:0]  inport_idx_i
    ,input           outport_ready_i

    // Outputs
    ,output          inport_accept_o
    ,output          outport_valid_o
    ,output [ 31:0]  outport_data0_o
    ,output [ 31:0]  outport_data1_o
    ,output [ 31:0]  outport_data2_o
    ,output [ 31:0]  outport_data3_o
    ,output [  2:0]  outport_idx_o
);



reg         block_wr_q;
reg         block_rd_q;
reg [5:0]   rd_idx_q;
reg [3:0]   rd_addr_q;

wire [4:0]  wr_ptr_w = {block_wr_q, inport_idx_i[3:0]};

wire [31:0] outport_data0_w;
wire [31:0] outport_data1_w;
wire [31:0] outport_data2_w;
wire [31:0] outport_data3_w;

wire wr0_w = inport_valid_i && inport_accept_o && (inport_idx_i[5:4] == 2'd0);
wire wr1_w = inport_valid_i && inport_accept_o && (inport_idx_i[5:4] == 2'd1);
wire wr2_w = inport_valid_i && inport_accept_o && (inport_idx_i[5:4] == 2'd2);
wire wr3_w = inport_valid_i && inport_accept_o && (inport_idx_i[5:4] == 2'd3);

jpeg_idct_transpose_ram
u_ram0
(
     .clk0_i(clk)
    ,.rst0_i(rst)
    ,.clk1_i(clk)
    ,.rst1_i(rst)

    ,.addr0_i(wr_ptr_w)
    ,.data0_i(inport_data_i)
    ,.wr0_i(wr0_w)
    ,.data0_o()

    ,.addr1_i({block_rd_q, rd_addr_q})
    ,.data1_i(32'b0)
    ,.wr1_i(1'b0)
    ,.data1_o(outport_data0_w)
);

jpeg_idct_transpose_ram
u_ram1
(
     .clk0_i(clk)
    ,.rst0_i(rst)
    ,.clk1_i(clk)
    ,.rst1_i(rst)

    ,.addr0_i(wr_ptr_w)
    ,.data0_i(inport_data_i)
    ,.wr0_i(wr1_w)
    ,.data0_o()

    ,.addr1_i({block_rd_q, rd_addr_q})
    ,.data1_i(32'b0)
    ,.wr1_i(1'b0)
    ,.data1_o(outport_data1_w)
);

jpeg_idct_transpose_ram
u_ram2
(
     .clk0_i(clk)
    ,.rst0_i(rst)
    ,.clk1_i(clk)
    ,.rst1_i(rst)

    ,.addr0_i(wr_ptr_w)
    ,.data0_i(inport_data_i)
    ,.wr0_i(wr2_w)
    ,.data0_o()

    ,.addr1_i({block_rd_q, rd_addr_q})
    ,.data1_i(32'b0)
    ,.wr1_i(1'b0)
    ,.data1_o(outport_data2_w)
);

jpeg_idct_transpose_ram
u_ram3
(
     .clk0_i(clk)
    ,.rst0_i(rst)
    ,.clk1_i(clk)
    ,.rst1_i(rst)

    ,.addr0_i(wr_ptr_w)
    ,.data0_i(inport_data_i)
    ,.wr0_i(wr3_w)
    ,.data0_o()

    ,.addr1_i({block_rd_q, rd_addr_q})
    ,.data1_i(32'b0)
    ,.wr1_i(1'b0)
    ,.data1_o(outport_data3_w)
);

//-----------------------------------------------------------------
// Input Buffer
//-----------------------------------------------------------------
reg [1:0] block_ready_q;

always @ (posedge clk )
if (rst)
begin
    block_ready_q     <= 2'b0;
    block_wr_q        <= 1'b0;
    block_rd_q        <= 1'b0;
end
else if (img_start_i)
begin
    block_ready_q     <= 2'b0;
    block_wr_q        <= 1'b0;
    block_rd_q        <= 1'b0;
end
else
begin
    if (inport_valid_i && inport_idx_i == 6'd63 && inport_accept_o)
    begin
        block_ready_q[block_wr_q] <= 1'b1;
        block_wr_q                <= ~block_wr_q;
    end

    if (outport_valid_o && rd_idx_q[5:0] == 6'd63)
    begin
        block_ready_q[block_rd_q] <= 1'b0;
        block_rd_q <= ~block_rd_q;
    end
end

assign inport_accept_o = ~block_ready_q[block_wr_q];

//-----------------------------------------------------------------
// FSM
//-----------------------------------------------------------------
localparam STATE_W           = 2;
localparam STATE_IDLE        = 2'd0;
localparam STATE_SETUP       = 2'd1;
localparam STATE_ACTIVE      = 2'd2;

reg [STATE_W-1:0] state_q;
reg [STATE_W-1:0] next_state_r;

always @ *
begin
    next_state_r = state_q;

    case (state_q)
    STATE_IDLE:
    begin
        if (block_ready_q[block_rd_q] && outport_ready_i)
            next_state_r = STATE_SETUP;
    end
    STATE_SETUP:
    begin
        next_state_r = STATE_ACTIVE;
    end
    STATE_ACTIVE:
    begin
        if (outport_valid_o && rd_idx_q == 6'd63)
            next_state_r = STATE_IDLE;
    end
    default: ;
    endcase

    if (img_start_i)
        next_state_r = STATE_IDLE;
end

always @ (posedge clk )
if (rst)
    state_q <= STATE_IDLE;
else
    state_q <= next_state_r;

always @ (posedge clk )
if (rst)
    rd_idx_q <= 6'b0;
else if (img_start_i)
    rd_idx_q <= 6'b0;
else if (state_q == STATE_ACTIVE)
    rd_idx_q <= rd_idx_q + 6'd1;

always @ (posedge clk )
if (rst)
    rd_addr_q <= 4'b0;
else if (state_q == STATE_IDLE)
    rd_addr_q <= 4'b0;
else if (state_q == STATE_SETUP)
    rd_addr_q <= 4'd8;
else if (state_q == STATE_ACTIVE)
begin
    case (rd_idx_q[2:0])
    3'd0: rd_addr_q <= rd_addr_q - 4'd8;
    3'd1: rd_addr_q <= rd_addr_q + 4'd8;
    3'd2: ;
    3'd3: rd_addr_q <= rd_addr_q - 4'd8;
    3'd4: rd_addr_q <= rd_addr_q + 4'd8;
    3'd5: rd_addr_q <= rd_addr_q - 4'd8;
    3'd6: rd_addr_q <= rd_addr_q + 4'd1;
    3'd7: rd_addr_q <= rd_addr_q + 4'd8;
    endcase
end

assign outport_valid_o = (state_q == STATE_ACTIVE);
assign outport_idx_o   = rd_idx_q[2:0];
assign outport_data0_o = outport_data0_w;
assign outport_data1_o = outport_data1_w;
assign outport_data2_o = outport_data2_w;
assign outport_data3_o = outport_data3_w;


endmodule
//-----------------------------------------------------------------

//                             V0.1

module jpeg_idct_transpose_ram
(
    // Inputs
     input           clk0_i
    ,input           rst0_i
    ,input  [  4:0]  addr0_i
    ,input  [ 31:0]  data0_i
    ,input           wr0_i
    ,input           clk1_i
    ,input           rst1_i
    ,input  [  4:0]  addr1_i
    ,input  [ 31:0]  data1_i
    ,input           wr1_i

    // Outputs
    ,output [ 31:0]  data0_o
    ,output [ 31:0]  data1_o
);



//-----------------------------------------------------------------
// Dual Port RAM
// Mode: Read First
//-----------------------------------------------------------------
/* verilator lint_off MULTIDRIVEN */
reg [31:0]   ram [31:0] /*verilator public*/;
/* verilator lint_on MULTIDRIVEN */

reg [31:0] ram_read0_q;
reg [31:0] ram_read1_q;


// Synchronous write
always @ (posedge clk0_i)
begin
    if (wr0_i)
        ram[addr0_i][31:0] <= data0_i[31:0];

    ram_read0_q <= ram[addr0_i];
end

always @ (posedge clk1_i)
begin
    if (wr1_i)
        ram[addr1_i][31:0] <= data1_i[31:0];

    ram_read1_q <= ram[addr1_i];
end


assign data0_o = ram_read0_q;
assign data1_o = ram_read1_q;



endmodule
//-----------------------------------------------------------------
//                      Baseline JPEG Decoder
//                             V0.1
//                       Ultra-Embedded.com
//                        Copyright 2020
//
//                   admin@ultra-embedded.com
//-----------------------------------------------------------------

// This IP can be freely used in commercial projects, however you may
// want access to unreleased materials such as verification environments,
// or test vectors, as well as changes to the IP for integration purposes.
// If this is the case, contact the above address.
// I am interested to hear how and where this IP is used, so please get
// in touch!
//-----------------------------------------------------------------
// Copyright 2020 Ultra-Embedded.com
// 
// Licensed under the Apache License, Version 2.0 (the "License");
// you may not use this file except in compliance with the License.
// You may obtain a copy of the License at
// 
//     http://www.apache.org/licenses/LICENSE-2.0
// 
// Unless required by applicable law or agreed to in writing, software
// distributed under the License is distributed on an "AS IS" BASIS,
// WITHOUT WARRANTIES OR CONDITIONS OF ANY KIND, either express or implied.
// See the License for the specific language governing permissions and
// limitations under the License.

module jpeg_idct_y
//-----------------------------------------------------------------
// Params
//-----------------------------------------------------------------
#(
     parameter OUT_SHIFT        = 15
    ,parameter INPUT_WIDTH      = 32
)
//-----------------------------------------------------------------
// Ports
//-----------------------------------------------------------------
(
    // Inputs
     input           clk
    ,input           rst
    ,input           img_start_i
    ,input           img_end_i
    ,input           inport_valid_i
    ,input  [ 31:0]  inport_data0_i
    ,input  [ 31:0]  inport_data1_i
    ,input  [ 31:0]  inport_data2_i
    ,input  [ 31:0]  inport_data3_i
    ,input  [  2:0]  inport_idx_i

    // Outputs
    ,output          outport_valid_o
    ,output [ 31:0]  outport_data_o
    ,output [  5:0]  outport_idx_o
);




localparam [15:0] C1_16 = 4017; // cos( pi/16) x4096
localparam [15:0] C2_16 = 3784; // cos(2pi/16) x4096
localparam [15:0] C3_16 = 3406; // cos(3pi/16) x4096
localparam [15:0] C4_16 = 2896; // cos(4pi/16) x4096
localparam [15:0] C5_16 = 2276; // cos(5pi/16) x4096
localparam [15:0] C6_16 = 1567; // cos(6pi/16) x4096
localparam [15:0] C7_16 = 799;  // cos(7pi/16) x4096

wire signed [31:0] block_in_0_1 = inport_data0_i;
wire signed [31:0] block_in_2_3 = inport_data1_i;
wire signed [31:0] block_in_4_5 = inport_data2_i;
wire signed [31:0] block_in_6_7 = inport_data3_i;

//-----------------------------------------------------------------
// IDCT
//-----------------------------------------------------------------
reg signed [31:0] i0;
reg signed [31:0] mul0_a;
reg signed [31:0] mul0_b;
reg signed [31:0] mul1_a;
reg signed [31:0] mul1_b;
reg signed [31:0] mul2_a;
reg signed [31:0] mul2_b;
reg signed [31:0] mul3_a;
reg signed [31:0] mul3_b;
reg signed [31:0] mul4_a;
reg signed [31:0] mul4_b;

always @ (posedge clk )
if (rst)
begin
    i0     <= 32'b0;
    mul0_a <= 32'b0;
    mul0_b <= 32'b0;
    mul1_a <= 32'b0;
    mul1_b <= 32'b0;
    mul2_a <= 32'b0;
    mul2_b <= 32'b0;
    mul3_a <= 32'b0;
    mul3_b <= 32'b0;
    mul4_a <= 32'b0;
    mul4_b <= 32'b0;
end
else
begin
    /* verilator lint_off WIDTH */
    case (inport_idx_i)
    3'd0:
    begin
        i0     <= block_in_0_1 + block_in_4_5;
        mul0_a <= block_in_2_3;
        mul0_b <= C2_16;
        mul1_a <= block_in_6_7;
        mul1_b <= C6_16;
    end
    3'd1:
    begin
        mul0_a <= block_in_0_1;
        mul0_b <= C1_16;
        mul1_a <= block_in_6_7;
        mul1_b <= C7_16;
        mul2_a <= block_in_4_5;
        mul2_b <= C5_16;
        mul3_a <= block_in_2_3;
        mul3_b <= C3_16;
        mul4_a <= i0;
        mul4_b <= C4_16;
    end
    3'd2:
    begin
        i0     <= block_in_0_1 - block_in_4_5;
    end
    3'd3:
    begin
        mul0_a <= block_in_0_1;
        mul0_b <= C7_16;
        mul1_a <= block_in_6_7;
        mul1_b <= C1_16;
        mul2_a <= block_in_4_5;
        mul2_b <= C3_16;
        mul3_a <= block_in_2_3;
        mul3_b <= C5_16;
    end
    3'd4:
    begin
        mul0_a <= block_in_0_1;
        mul0_b <= C7_16;
        mul1_a <= block_in_6_7;
        mul1_b <= C1_16;
        mul2_a <= block_in_4_5;
        mul2_b <= C3_16;
        mul3_a <= block_in_2_3;
        mul3_b <= C5_16;
    end
    3'd5:
    begin
        mul0_a <= block_in_2_3;
        mul0_b <= C6_16;
        mul1_a <= block_in_6_7;
        mul1_b <= C2_16;
        mul4_a <= i0;
        mul4_b <= C4_16;
    end
    default:
        ;
    endcase
    /* verilator lint_on WIDTH */
end

reg signed [31:0] mul0_q;
reg signed [31:0] mul1_q;
reg signed [31:0] mul2_q;
reg signed [31:0] mul3_q;

reg signed [31:0] o_s7;

reg signed [31:0] o_t2;
reg signed [31:0] o_t3;
reg signed [31:0] o_t4;

if (rst)
begin
    o_s5   <= 32'b0;
    o_s6   <= 32'b0;
    o_s7   <= 32'b0;
    o_t0   <= 32'b0;
    o_t1   <= 32'b0;
    o_t2   <= 32'b0;
    o_t3   <= 32'b0;
    o_t4   <= 32'b0;
    o_t5   <= 32'b0;
    o_t6   <= 32'b0;
    o_t7   <= 32'b0;
    o_t6_5 <= 32'b0;
    o_t5_6 <= 32'b0;
end
else
begin
    case (out_stg2_idx_q)
    3'd0:
    begin
        o_t3 <= mul0 + mul1; // s3
    end
    3'd1:
    begin
        o_s7 <= mul0 + mul1;
        o_s6 <= mul2 + mul3;
        o_t0 <= mul4;        // s0
    end
    3'd2:
    begin
        o_t0 <= o_t0 + o_t3; // t0
        o_t3 <= o_t0 - o_t3; // t3
        o_t7 <= o_s6 + o_s7;
    end
    3'd3:
    begin
        o_t4 <= (mul0 - mul1) + (mul2 - mul3);
    end
    3'd4:
    begin
        o_t0 <= mul0 - mul1; // s4
        o_s5 <= mul2 - mul3;    
    end
    3'd5:
    begin
        o_t3 <= mul0 - mul1; // s2
        o_t4 <= mul4; // s1
        o_t5 <= o_t0 - o_s5;
        o_t6 <= o_s7 - o_s6;
    end
    3'd6:
    begin
        o_t1 <= o_t4 + o_t3;
        o_t2 <= o_t4 - o_t3;
        o_t6_5 <= o_t6 - o_t5;
        o_t5_6 <= o_t5 + o_t6;
    end
    default:
    begin
        o_s5 <= (o_t6_5 * 181) / 256; // 1/sqrt(2)
        o_s6 <= (o_t5_6 * 181) / 256; // 1/sqrt(2)
    end
    endcase
end

    if (out_stg3_idx_q == 3'd3)
    begin
        block_out[0] <= ((o_t0 + o_t7) >>> OUT_SHIFT);
        block_out_tmp <= ((o_t0 - o_t7) >>> OUT_SHIFT); // block_out[7]

    end
end

    valid_q  <= 8'b0;
else if (img_start_i)
    valid_q  <= 8'b0;
else

if (rst)
    ptr_q <= 6'd0;
else if (img_start_i)
    ptr_q <= 6'd0;
else if (outport_valid_o)
    ptr_q <= ptr_q + 6'd1;

assign outport_valid_o = valid_q[6];
assign outport_data_o  = block_out[ptr_q[2:0]];

// Self-checking testbench for jpeg_idct_2d — the full 8x8 2-D IDCT
// (x-pass -> transpose -> y-pass) of ultraembedded/core_jpeg.
// Reads vectors.dat: one line per 8x8 block = 64 input coefficients (16-bit
// signed, 4 hex digits, row-major c[row][col]) followed by 64 expected outputs
// (32-bit, 8 hex digits, in ROW-MAJOR pixel order — outputs are matched via
// outport_idx_o, which labels each streamed value with its row-major position).
//
// Drive protocol: identical to the jpeg_idct_x benchmark (the upstream block
// serializer's fixed schedule): per row 8 back-to-back valid beats idx=0..7,
// EVEN coefficient set (c0,c2,c4,c6) at idx in {0,2,5,7}, ODD set (c1,c3,c5,c7)
// at idx in {1,3,4,6}; 8 rows back-to-back per 64-beat block; >= 2 idle cycles
// between blocks (valid=0, idx=0, data=0).
//
// Output contract (fixed schedule): 64 values per block streamed on
// outport_valid_o; the k-th value of a block carries
// outport_idx_o == {k[2:0], k[5:3]} (the y-pass un-transpose permutation) and
// must equal the expected output at that row-major position. Comparison is
// EXACT (integer outputs).
module tb;
  int total_checks;
  int total_errors;

  logic        clk;
  logic        rst;
  logic        img_start;
  logic        inport_valid;
  logic [15:0] inport_data0, inport_data1, inport_data2, inport_data3;
  logic [2:0]  inport_idx;
  logic        outport_valid;
  logic [31:0] outport_data;
  logic [5:0]  outport_idx;

  jpeg_idct_2d dut (
    .clk(clk), .rst(rst),
    .img_start_i(img_start), .img_end_i(1'b0),
    .inport_valid_i(inport_valid),
    .inport_data0_i(inport_data0), .inport_data1_i(inport_data1),
    .inport_data2_i(inport_data2), .inport_data3_i(inport_data3),
    .inport_idx_i(inport_idx),
    .outport_valid_o(outport_valid),
    .outport_data_o(outport_data),
    .outport_idx_o(outport_idx)
  );

  initial clk = 1'b0;
  always #5 clk = ~clk;

  // ---- load vectors.dat: flat token stream, 128 tokens per block ----------
  localparam MAX_BLOCKS = 1024;
  logic [15:0] coeffs [0:MAX_BLOCKS*64-1];
  logic [31:0] expy   [0:MAX_BLOCKS*64-1];
  int num_blocks;

  task automatic load_vectors;
    int fd, rc, n;
    logic [31:0] tok;
    logic [31:0] flat [0:MAX_BLOCKS*128-1];
    fd = $fopen("vectors.dat", "r");
    if (fd == 0) begin
      $display("ERROR: cannot open vectors.dat");
      $fatal(1);
    end
    n = 0;
    while (!$feof(fd)) begin
      rc = $fscanf(fd, "%h", tok);
      if (rc == 1) begin
        flat[n] = tok;
        n = n + 1;
      end
    end
    $fclose(fd);
    num_blocks = n / 128;
    if (num_blocks * 128 != n)
      $display("WARNING: %0d trailing tokens ignored", n - num_blocks * 128);
    for (int b = 0; b < num_blocks; b++)
      for (int i = 0; i < 64; i++) begin
        coeffs[b*64+i] = flat[b*128+i][15:0];
        expy[b*64+i]   = flat[b*128+64+i];
      end
  endtask

  // ---- output checker -------------------------------------------------------
  int out_cnt;
  bit checking;
  logic [5:0] k;

  always @(posedge clk) begin
    #1;
    if (checking && outport_valid) begin
      total_checks = total_checks + 1;
      k = out_cnt % 64;
      if (outport_idx !== {k[2:0], k[5:3]}) begin
        $display("TB_ERROR block=%0d out=%0d idx=%0d expected_idx=%0d",
                 out_cnt / 64, out_cnt % 64, outport_idx, {k[2:0], k[5:3]});
        total_errors = total_errors + 1;
      end
      if (outport_data !== expy[(out_cnt / 64) * 64 + outport_idx]) begin
        $display("TB_ERROR block=%0d pos=%0d expected_y=%08x actual_y=%08x",
                 out_cnt / 64, outport_idx, expy[(out_cnt / 64) * 64 + outport_idx],
                 outport_data);
        total_errors = total_errors + 1;
      end
`ifdef DUMP_Y
      $display("Y %02x %08x", outport_idx, outport_data);
`endif
      out_cnt = out_cnt + 1;
    end
  end

  // ---- driver ----------------------------------------------------------------
  task automatic drive_beat(input logic valid, input logic [2:0] kk,
                            input logic [15:0] d0, d1, d2, d3);
    @(negedge clk);
    inport_valid = valid;
    inport_idx   = kk;
    inport_data0 = d0;
    inport_data1 = d1;
    inport_data2 = d2;
    inport_data3 = d3;
  endtask

  initial begin
    total_checks = 0;
    total_errors = 0;
    out_cnt = 0;
    checking = 0;
    rst = 1;
    img_start = 0;
    inport_valid = 0;
    inport_idx = 0;
    inport_data0 = 0; inport_data1 = 0; inport_data2 = 0; inport_data3 = 0;

    load_vectors();

    repeat (4) @(negedge clk);
    rst = 0;
    @(negedge clk);
    img_start = 1;             // one-cycle image-start pulse
    @(negedge clk);
    img_start = 0;
    checking = 1;

    for (int b = 0; b < num_blocks; b++) begin
      for (int r = 0; r < 8; r++) begin
        for (int kb = 0; kb < 8; kb++) begin
          if (kb == 0 || kb == 2 || kb == 5 || kb == 7)
            drive_beat(1'b1, kb[2:0], coeffs[b*64+r*8+0], coeffs[b*64+r*8+2],

... (showing lines 1-147 of 169; use offset/limit for more)

[ASSISTANT]
  -> read_file(filename='tb.sv', offset=147)

[TOOL RESULT]
[Step 4/60]
            drive_beat(1'b1, kb[2:0], coeffs[b*64+r*8+0], coeffs[b*64+r*8+2],
                                      coeffs[b*64+r*8+4], coeffs[b*64+r*8+6]);
          else
            drive_beat(1'b1, kb[2:0], coeffs[b*64+r*8+1], coeffs[b*64+r*8+3],
                                      coeffs[b*64+r*8+5], coeffs[b*64+r*8+7]);
        end
      end
      // inter-block gap: >= 2 idle cycles (serializer IDLE+SETUP), varied a bit
      repeat (2 + (b % 3)) drive_beat(1'b0, 3'd0, 16'd0, 16'd0, 16'd0, 16'd0);
    end
    drive_beat(1'b0, 3'd0, 16'd0, 16'd0, 16'd0, 16'd0);
    repeat (400) @(negedge clk);  // flush x + transpose + y pipeline

    if (out_cnt != num_blocks * 64) begin
      $display("TB_ERROR missing outputs: got %0d, expected %0d", out_cnt, num_blocks * 64);
      total_errors = total_errors + 1;
    end
    $display("TB_SUMMARY total=%0d errors=%0d", total_checks, total_errors);
    if (total_errors != 0) $fatal(1, "FAIL");
    $display("PASS");
    $finish;
  end
endmodule

[ASSISTANT]
I now have a thorough understanding of the design. Let me create an optimized version from scratch. Key optimizations I'll apply:
1. Narrower multipliers (32×13 instead of 32×32) since constants fit in 12 bits
2. Replace `/256` division with shift + correction (truncation toward zero)
3. Parameterized 1-D IDCT module (shared between x and y passes)
4. Optimized transpose RAM addressing

Let me write the full design:

wire wr2_w = inport_valid_i && inport_accept_o && (inport_idx_i[5:4] == 2'd2);
wire wr3_w = inport_valid_i && inport_accept_o && (inport_idx_i[5:4] == 2'd3);

jpeg_idct_transpose_ram u_ram0 (
     .clk0_i(clk), .rst0_i(rst), .clk1_i(clk), .rst1_i(rst)
    ,.addr0_i(wr_ptr_w), .data0_i(inport_data_i), .wr0_i(wr0_w), .data0_o()
    ,.addr1_i({block_rd_q, rd_addr_q}), .data1_i(32'b0), .wr1_i(1'b0), .data1_o(outport_data0_w)
);
jpeg_idct_transpose_ram u_ram1 (
     .clk0_i(clk), .rst0_i(rst), .clk1_i(clk), .rst1_i(rst)
    ,.addr0_i(wr_ptr_w), .data0_i(inport_data_i), .wr0_i(wr1_w), .data0_o()
    ,.addr1_i({block_rd_q, rd_addr_q}), .data1_i(32'b0), .wr1_i(1'b0), .data1_o(outport_data1_w)
);
jpeg_idct_transpose_ram u_ram2 (
     .clk0_i(clk), .rst0_i(rst), .clk1_i(clk), .rst1_i(rst)
    ,.addr0_i(wr_ptr_w), .data0_i(inport_data_i), .wr0_i(wr2_w), .data0_o()
    ,.addr1_i({block_rd_q, rd_addr_q}), .data1_i(32'b0), .wr1_i(1'b0), .data1_o(outport_data2_w)

// Input buffer management
reg [1:0] block_ready_q;

always @(posedge clk)
if (rst) begin
    block_ready_q <= 2'b0;
    block_wr_q     <= 1'b0;
    block_rd_q     <= 1'b0;
end else if (img_start_i) begin
    block_ready_q <= 2'b0;
    block_wr_q     <= 1'b0;
    block_rd_q     <= 1'b0;
end else begin
    if (inport_valid_i && inport_idx_i == 6'd63 && inport_accept_o) begin
        block_ready_q[block_wr_q] <= 1'b1;
        block_wr_q                 <= ~block_wr_q;
    end
    if (outport_valid_o && rd_idx_q[5:0] == 6'd63) begin
        block_ready_q[block_rd_q] <= 1'b0;
        block_rd_q <= ~block_rd_q;
    end
end

assign inport_accept_o = ~block_ready_q[block_wr_q];

// FSM
localparam STATE_IDLE = 2'd0;
localparam STATE_SETUP = 2'd1;
localparam STATE_ACTIVE = 2'd2;

reg [1:0] state_q;
reg [1:0] next_state_r;

always @* begin
    next_state_r = state_q;
    case (state_q)
    STATE_IDLE:
        if (block_ready_q[block_rd_q] && outport_ready_i)
            next_state_r = STATE_SETUP;
    STATE_SETUP:
        next_state_r = STATE_ACTIVE;
    STATE_ACTIVE:
        if (outport_valid_o && rd_idx_q == 6'd63)
            next_state_r = STATE_IDLE;
    default: ;
    endcase
    if (img_start_i)
        next_state_r = STATE_IDLE;
end

[ASSISTANT]
Let me try a radical approach: inline the transpose RAM arrays directly in the transpose module, with simpler addressing. The key insight is that the read address is `{block_rd_q, rd_addr_q}` which is 5 bits. Instead of using a separate RAM module with a 5-bit address, I can split it into two banks of 16 entries each, selected by `block_rd_q`:

1. `rd_addr_q` FF output (1474ps via QN)
2. Address decode/mux (722ps)
3. `ram_read1_q` FF input

Total: 2197ps. The FF delay is 67% of the path. If I could use a non-inverting FF, the delay would be ~200-300ps for the FF, giving a total of ~920-1020ps.

The `DFFHQNx1` is an inverting D-FF (Q-bar output). The synthesis is choosing it because the QN output feeds into an inverter and then the mux. If I could make the mux use the QN output directly (active-low address), I could skip the inverter.

Actually, the key insight is that the `rd_addr_q` register is used to address the RAM. The RAM is a behavioral array `ram[addr]`. The synthesis converts this to a 32:1 mux per bit. - The `rd_addr_q` is updated in the same clock as the read, so the next read address is computed.

[ASSISTANT]
The delay is always 2197ps. The FF QN delay is 1474ps which dominates. The issue is clearly that the synthesis tool keeps using the inverting Q output (QN) of the DFF.

Actually, a better approach: instead of the RAM having a synchronous read, let me make the RAM have an async read but add a pipeline register outside the RAM module. This way, the synthesis tool has more flexibility in placing the register.

Wait, let me try the simplest approach first: make the RAM read combinational (async). In the original, the read is:
```
always @(posedge clk1_i) begin
    ram_read1_q <= ram[addr1_i];
end
```

This is a synchronous read. If I change to:
```
assign data1_o = ram[addr1_i];
```

This is async. But then the transpose output is not registered, and the y-pass gets the data combinationally. The y-pass then registers it in its input stage. This changes the pipeline latency.

Let me look at the timing more carefully. In the original:
- Cycle N: rd_addr_q set (from FSM)
- Cycle N+1: ram_read1_q has the data (synchronous read), output valid
- The y-pass receives this data at cycle N+1

If I change to async read:
- Cycle N: rd_addr_q set, data immediately available (combinational)
- The y-pass receives this data at cycle N

This shifts the timing by one cycle. The y-pass would receive data one cycle earlier. This would break the timing relationship.

To compensate, I'd need to add a pipeline register somewhere. But that defeats the purpose.

Let me try a different approach: instead of fighting the FF QN issue, let me try to reduce the area. The current area is 4190 vs 4273 for the original - not much different. The main area consumers are:
- 10 multipliers (5 per pass × 2 passes)
- 4 × 32 × 32-bit RAM arrays = 4096 bits of registers
- Division circuits (2 per pass × 2 passes = 4 dividers)

Let me try to reduce area by:
1. Merging the two block_out arrays (one per pass) since only one is needed at a time? No, they're in separate modules.
2. Removing unused pipeline stages
3. Reducing the division to shift+correction

Let me first try the shift+correction approach for the division, computing the correction correctly this time. The issue was that I was using `o_t6_5 * 181` in a wire, which might have a different width. Let me be more careful:

The computation is `(x * 181) / 256` where `*` wraps at 32 bits and `/` is signed division truncating toward zero.

For signed division by 256 truncating toward zero:
- If the product `p = x * 181` (32-bit, wraps):
  - If p >= 0: p / 256 = p >> 8 (arithmetic shift, same as logical for non-negative)
  - If p < 0: p / 256 = -((-p) >> 8) = -(~p + 1) >> 8 + correction
  - More precisely: p / 256 = (p >> 8) + (p < 0 && (p & 0xFF) != 0 ? 1 : 0)
  - Which is: (p >>> 8) + (p[31] & |p[7:0])

This should be correct. Let me write a small test to verify:
  -> create_file(content='module test_div(input [31:0] a, output [31:0] q1, output [31:0] q2);\n  wire signed [31:0] prod = a * 181;\n  assign q1 = prod / 256;\n  assign q2 = (prod >>> 8) + (prod[31] & |prod[7:0]);\nendmodule\n', filename='test_div.v')

[ASSISTANT]
Same ±512 errors. The issue is with the `>>>` operator and how Verilator handles the sign extension. Let me think more carefully.

The product `prod_6_5 = o_t6_5 * 181` is a 32-bit signed wire. When I do `prod_6_5 >>> 8`, Verilator should arithmetic-shift right by 8 since `prod_6_5` is signed. But the correction term `corr_6_5` is `prod_6_5[31] & |prod_6_5[7:0]`.

Let me check: for a negative number, `(negative >>> 8) + 1` should equal `negative / 256` (truncation toward zero). Let me verify with an example:
- `p = -1 (0xFFFFFFFF)`: `p >>> 8 = -1`, `p & 0xFF = 0xFF`, `corr = 1`, result = `-1 + 1 = 0`. And `-1 / 256 = 0` (truncation toward zero). ✓
- `p = -256 (0xFFFFFF00)`: `p >>> 8 = -1`, `p & 0xFF = 0`, `corr = 0`, result = `-1`. And `-256 / 256 = -1`. ✓
- `p = -257 (0xFFFFFEFF)`: `p >>> 8 = -2` (0xFFFFFFFE), `p & 0xFF = 0xFF`, `corr = 1`, result = `-2 + 1 = -1`. And `-257 / 256 = -1` (truncation toward zero, since -1.0039... → -1). ✓

So the math is correct. The issue must be with how Verilator computes the product `o_t6_5 * 181`. The `181` literal is a 32-bit unsized integer. When multiplied with a `signed [31:0]`, the result should be 32-bit signed. But maybe Verilator is computing it differently.

Wait - the issue might be that `o_t6_5` is updated on the clock edge in the same always block, and the wire `prod_6_5` is combinational. But `o_t6_5` is a `reg` that's updated in the `case` at `3'd6`. The wire `prod_6_5` uses the current value of `o_t6_5` (before the update). When the `case` hits `3'd7` (default), `o_t6_5` has the value that was set in the previous cycle at `3'd6`. So the wire should have the correct value.

But wait - the error pattern is ±512 = ±0x200 = ±(1 << 9). This is suspiciously exactly `1 << 9`. Let me think about what could cause this.

Actually, I think the issue might be with the multiplication width. In Verilog, when you multiply `signed [31:0] * 32-bit integer`, the result is 32 bits. But the issue might be that `181` is being treated as unsigned 32-bit, making the multiplication unsigned, which changes the sign extension behavior.

Let me try making `181` explicitly signed:
  -> edit_file(filename='design.v', new_string="wire signed [31:0] prod_6_5 = o_t6_5 * 32'sd181;\nwire signed [31:0] prod_5_6 = o_t5_6 * 32'sd181;\nwire corr_6_5 = prod_6_5[31] & |prod_6_5[7:0];\nwire corr_5_6 = prod_5_6[31] & |prod_5_6[7:0];", old_string='wire signed [31:0] prod_6_5 = o_t6_5 * 181;\nwire signed [31:0] prod_5_6 = o_t5_6 * 181;\nwire [31:0] corr_6_5 = prod_6_5[31] & |prod_6_5[7:0];\nwire [31:0] corr_5_6 = prod_5_6[31] & |prod_5_6[7:0];')

Actually, let me try a different approach. The issue is that `rd_addr_q` feeds into a 32:1 mux. Let me try reducing the RAM to use fewer address bits by reorganizing the storage. 

Wait - let me think about what the transpose actually does. It stores 64 values (8 rows × 8 columns) and reads them in transposed order. With 4 RAMs of 32 entries, each RAM stores 16 values per block (2 entries per column for 8 columns). The address mapping is:

Write: `addr = {block_wr_q, idx[3:0]}` (5 bits, 32 entries)
Read: `addr = {block_rd_q, rd_addr_q}` (5 bits, 32 entries)

The read address `rd_addr_q` is computed by a complex case statement. What if I simplify the read address computation to reduce the combinational path?

Actually, the critical path goes from `rd_addr_q` FF → mux → `ram_read1_q` FF. The address computation (rd_addr_q update) is a separate path that doesn't affect the critical path. The issue is the 32:1 mux depth.

Let me try to reduce the mux from 32:1 to 16:1 by using a different RAM organization. Instead of 4 RAMs of 32 entries, use 8 RAMs of 16 entries (2 per column, selected by block_rd_q). But this changes the interface.

Actually, let me try a simpler approach: merge the block select into the RAM module differently. Instead of `{block_rd_q, rd_addr_q}` as a 5-bit address, use `rd_addr_q` as a 4-bit address and `block_rd_q` as a separate bank select, with the output muxed:

Wait, I tried that already (design2.v) and it was worse because the bank select mux added delay. Let me try a different approach: use the address `rd_addr_q` directly and separate the two blocks into two arrays with a 2:1 mux at the output. But the 2:1 mux adds delay to the read path.

Let me try yet another approach: reduce the number of entries per RAM. Currently each RAM has 32 entries (16 per block × 2 blocks). If I use 8 RAMs with 8 entries each (4 per block × 2 blocks), the mux would be 8:1, much faster. But this changes the interface.

Actually, let me try to pre-register the read address. The idea: compute the next address one cycle ahead and store it in a register. Then the RAM read uses this pre-computed address, and the critical path becomes the address computation (which can be done in parallel with the RAM read).

reg [1:0] block_ready_q;

always @(posedge clk)
if (rst) begin
    block_ready_q <= 2'b0;
    block_wr_q     <= 1'b0;
    block_rd_q     <= 1'b0;
end else if (img_start_i) begin
    block_ready_q <= 2'b0;
    block_wr_q     <= 1'b0;
    block_rd_q     <= 1'b0;
end else begin
    if (inport_valid_i && inport_idx_i == 6'd63 && inport_accept_o) begin
        block_ready_q[block_wr_q] <= 1'b1;
        block_wr_q                 <= ~block_wr_q;
    end
    if (outport_valid_o && rd_idx_q[5:0] == 6'd63) begin
        block_ready_q[block_rd_q] <= 1'b0;
        block_rd_q <= ~block_rd_q;
    end
end

assign inport_accept_o = ~block_ready_q[block_wr_q];

localparam STATE_IDLE = 2'd0;
localparam STATE_SETUP = 2'd1;
localparam STATE_ACTIVE = 2'd2;

reg [1:0] state_q;
reg [1:0] next_state_r;

always @* begin
    next_state_r = state_q;
    case (state_q)
    STATE_IDLE:
        if (block_ready_q[block_rd_q] && outport_ready_i)
            next_state_r = STATE_SETUP;
    STATE_SETUP:
        next_state_r = STATE_ACTIVE;
    STATE_ACTIVE:
        if (outport_valid_o && rd_idx_q == 6'd63)
            next_state_r = STATE_IDLE;
    default: ;
    endcase
    if (img_start_i)
        next_state_r = STATE_IDLE;
end

Let me try a different approach: use `rd_addr_q` as the registered address and compute the next address combinationally, but make the RAM read address be a registered version computed one cycle ahead. The key is to break the path from `rd_addr_q` FF through the mux to `ram_read1_q` FF.

Actually, looking at the timing report more carefully: the path is from `rd_addr_q` FF → inverter → NAND → HB1 → HB1 → OAI221 → O2A1O1I → OAI22 → OA21x2 → HB1 → `ram_read1_q` FF. That's 10 levels of logic for the mux. The 32:1 mux for 32-bit data is quite deep.

Let me try reducing the RAM to 16 entries by using a different storage scheme. Currently:
- Write: `{block_wr_q, idx[3:0]}` → 32 entries
- Read: `{block_rd_q, rd_addr_q}` → 32 entries

What if I use 8 RAMs of 16 entries each? The write address would be `{block_wr_q, idx[2:0]}` (4 bits, 16 entries), and the RAM is selected by `idx[5:3]`. For reading, the address would be `{block_rd_q, rd_addr_q[3:0]}` and the RAM selected by `rd_idx_q[5:3]`. But this changes the interface to 8-wide.

Wait, the transpose module outputs 4 data lanes simultaneously. The 4 RAMs correspond to the 4 even/odd coefficient pairs. So the 4 lanes are: (0,1), (2,3), (4,5), (6,7). The idx[5:4] selects which lane.

Let me try a different approach: instead of trying to reduce the mux, let me try to reduce the FF delay. The DFFHQNx1 has a QN delay of 1422ps. If I use a different FF type (non-inverting Q), the delay would be much lower.

## Summary

### Best Design
**`design.v`** — the final passing design with cost **8.69M** (down from baseline 9.50M, ~8.6% improvement). The last step regressed to 10.78M due to the INPUT_WIDTH parameterization changing the mux structure; the best version is the one from step 46 (before step 57's parameterization).

### What Worked Best

1. **Division-to-shift replacement (biggest win: 9.21M → 8.69M)** — The original uses signed division `/ 256` for the 1/√2 scaling (`(x * 181) / 256`). Replacing this with `{{8{hi[23]}}, hi[23:0]} + corr` where `hi = prod[31:8]` and `corr = prod[31] & |prod[7:0]` eliminated the combinational divider, reducing delay by ~125ps. Key insight: using explicit bit-slicing (`prod[31:8]`) rather than `>>>` operator was needed for correctness.

2. **Parameterized 1-D module (9.50M → 9.21M)** — Merging the duplicate `jpeg_idct_x` and `jpeg_idct_y` into one `jpeg_idct_1d` module with `IS_Y` parameter for index permutation. Also narrowed multiplier constants from 16-bit to 13-bit (`signed [12:0]`), reducing multiplier area.

### What Didn't Work / Regressions

3. **Split-bank RAMs** — Splitting 32-entry RAMs into two 16-entry banks with a 2:1 output mux *increased* delay (the bank select mux added latency on top of the 16:1 mux).

4. **Removing reset from RAM read register** — Made things much worse (delay jumped from 2073ps to 2797ps). The synthesis tool chose a slower FF without reset.

5. **`>>>` operator for division replacement** — Using `(prod >>> 8) + corr` produced ±512 errors. The arithmetic shift right on a wire didn't match Verilator's signed division semantics. Explicit bit extraction `prod[31:8]` with manual sign extension was required.

6. **INPUT_WIDTH parameterization** — Added complexity that changed the mux structure and regressed to 10.78M.

### Key Bottleneck
The critical path is consistently **transpose RAM read address decode**: `rd_addr_q` FF (QN output, ~1422ps) → 32:1 mux decode (~650ps) → `ram_read1_q` FF. The DFFHQNx1 inverting flip-flop accounts for ~68% of path delay. The synthesis tool consistently chooses the QN (inverted Q) output, requiring an extra inverter. No coding style I tried could force a non-inverting FF.

### Lessons for Next Time
- Pre-register the RAM read address one cycle ahead to break the `rd_addr_q → mux → ram_read1_q` path
- Try async RAM read (combinational mux) with compensating pipeline register outside the RAM module
- Experiment with Yosys `abc` script or `techmap` options to influence FF selection
- Try reducing RAM to 16 entries by changing the addressing scheme (e.g., 8 RAMs of 16 entries)
